[Project guide](community/projects/tools/tb-spam-guard.md).
- [specpi-jev-guard](https://github.com/TannerMidd/specpi-jev-guard) - Pi extension: local deny/fast-pass rules, then TypeSafe Jev danger scores for uncertain shell/file commands. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/specpi-jev-guard.md).
- [spendcoder (GL-code corporate card spend from your own policy; models judge, code decides)](https://github.com/ansh-shah/spendcoder) - Turns a corporate-card export into GL-coded transactions using your categories, rule matrix and vendor overrides: code handles arithmetic, eligibility, amount ranges and row preservation, while small bounded questions (category, which matching rule, review flags) go to a decision model, Jev by default via OpenRouter Decisions. Every row gets an audit trail and uncertain rows go to a review queue. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/spendcoder.md).
- [SPINACH Lab (knowledge-graph question answering with Jev-guided navigation)](https://github.com/arturoeanton/go-r2-SPINACH) - Go reimplementation of the SPINACH knowledge-base question-answering research with an interactive graph editor, a step-by-step educational question journey and a lightweight SPARQL engine (SPARQLite); its experimental extension uses Jev as a navigation layer that ranks subgraphs before each model action, while OpenAI writes the answers. Live demo; English and Spanish docs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/spinach-lab.md).
- [sports-image-sorter](https://github.com/nematatu/sports-image-sorter-local) - Python CLI and review UI that sorts sports photos into per-sport folders with local vision engines and conservative fusion, with an optional Jev cloud engine for image Choice questions behind explicit off-device gates. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sports-image-sorter.md).
- [Spotlight (Buried Signals)](https://github.com/buriedsignals/spotlight) - OSINT investigation orchestrator for journalists working with AI agents, with opt-in decision checks via OpenRouter (default `typesafe/jev-1.13`) that flag findings their stored sources do not support, report prose that overstates findings, and knowledge-base drift — flags can only lower confidence. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/spotlight.md).
- [Spring AI TypeSafe](https://github.com/spring-ai-community/spring-ai-typesafe) - Unofficial Java/Spring AI System One client with JevJudge, guardrail/RAG advisors, and starter (Maven `org.springaicommunity`; distinct from jev-java). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/spring-ai-typesafe.md).
- [sq (System One Query)](https://github.com/LIghtJUNction/sq) - Rust CLI and local relay that runs Choice, Score and Noul decisions over shell pipelines with bounded concurrency and ordered JSON-line output, for Jev, Drex or a local Laya server, plus a typed Python SDK. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sq-system-one-query.md).
- [SREGym](https://github.com/SREGym/SREGym) - Platform and benchmark for AI agents that resolve production incidents in live Kubernetes environments (90 SRE problems, SREGym-Lite starter set), with optional Jev decision support that reviews an agent's diagnostic tests and submissions using evidence sent to TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sregym.md).
- [SSO as a Decision Model (Rust OpenID AuthZEN policy decision point with Jev judging device, location and time risk)](https://github.com/neko32/sso_as_decision_model) - Rust policy decision point that speaks OpenID AuthZEN and treats every SSO login as a decision: hard rules in Rust handle identity, policy, role and business hours in about 10 ms, while Jev rates device trust, location anomaly, unusual time and overall risk. Every response carries a decision trace saying who judged each factor and why. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sso-as-decision-model.md).
- [stable-computer-use (precise macOS computer use from the accessibility tree, with Jev as an optional step)](https://github.com/zhangyinglong3550/stable-computer-use) - macOS computer use for any AI agent that reads the accessibility tree and gives every element a reference that stays stable across snapshots, acting by reference instead of pixel coordinates; a six-tier decision ladder tries fast local scoring first and only asks Jev (closed choice among up to eight candidates) or your own LLM when local scoring is unsure, saving confident Jev answers to memory. Sister project of stable-chrome; Chinese and English docs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stable-computer-use.md).
- [stalwart-jev (Stalwart mail spam classifier answered by Jev)](https://github.com/mikluko/stalwart-jev) - Small Go service that answers the Stalwart mail server's LLM spam classifier (SpamLlm) from Jev: one choice question over subject and body returns Unsolicited, Commercial, Harmful or Legitimate with a confidence and probability. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stalwart-jev.md).
- [StanceJEV (paper code: zero-shot stance detection as a typed Jev choice)](https://github.com/danikstifloanpaarnatl/jev-stance) - Paper code that treats zero-shot stance detection as a typed choice over favor, against and none: context reconstruction and batch reasoning by an LLM feed a Jev decision, and a local consistency check verifies it; the README reports results on SemEval-2016 and VAST with two remote calls per item. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stancejev.md).
- [standard_model_for_jev_tasks (jev-shim)](https://github.com/NeuralNotwerk/standard_model_for_jev_tasks) - Dependency-free shim that serves an ordinary instruct LLM (vLLM or llama.cpp) behind TypeSafe's `POST /v1/systemone` interface, constraining every answer with a GBNF grammar and reading probabilities from logits, so Jev clients and JevBench can drive a self-hosted model. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/standard-model-for-jev-tasks.md).
- [Stanley Code](https://github.com/devagrawal09/stanley-code) - Routes coding requests into bounded Jev review and triage workflows, with trusted repository extensions and an optional Pi coding-agent fallback. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stanley-code.md).
- [StartLux-Decision](https://github.com/StartLuxLabs/StartLux-Decision) - Independent open typed decision models (0.8B to 35B-A3B, text and images) served on the TypeSafe `/v1/systemone` request format, with inference code, evaluation scripts, and game logs; not affiliated with TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/startlux-decision.md).
- [stil-lint](https://github.com/fredrsat/stil-lint) - Style and quality linter for Norwegian (and English) text, delivered as a CLI and MCP server, that flags prose reading like unedited LLM output through several independent layers, with TypeSafe Jev as the optional judgment layer (directly or via OpenRouter); a style linter, not an authorship detector. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stil-lint.md).
- [stingray](https://github.com/Nanako0129/stingray) - Claude Code/Codex Stop hook: TypeSafe Jev catches half-done turns (no action, broken promise, empty watch, wrong language). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stingray.md).
- [StockSignal](https://github.com/kingabzpro/StocksSignal) - Terminal dashboard that combines yfinance data and Olostep news with TypeSafe Jev choice and score questions to show BUY/HOLD/SELL probabilities for up to five stocks (not financial advice). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stocksignal.md).
- [stop-rules](https://github.com/haystackeditor/stop-rules) - Coding-agent stop hook that asks TypeSafe Jev yes/no per changed piece against written team rules (multi-agent; optional team server). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stop-rules.md).
- [Strands Decider](https://github.com/strands-labs/strands-decider) - Run a small fast System One–style decision model for agentic option/score/yes-no workflows (independent of hosted Jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/strands-decider.md).
- [strapi-plugin-jev-review](https://github.com/gbesse/strapi-plugin-jev-review) - Strapi 5 editorial review/publish guard: TypeSafe Jev chooses approve/escalate/revise with confidence. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/strapi-plugin-jev-review.md).
- [stuntd](https://github.com/bladedevoff/stuntd) - Local Jev-compatible proxy that serves or learns typed System One decisions on a Laya head (optional OpenAI/Jev upstream). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/stuntd.md).
- [Sudus](https://github.com/eas4ai/sudus) - Plugin (Claude Code, Codex, Muse; formerly Cairn) that keeps AI-assisted development tied to agreed scope, with an optional TypeSafe Jev evaluator (`typesafeai.enabled`) that measures the agent's draft at Consequential choices on five weighted dimensions instead of a harness review model. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sudus.md).
- [super (superpowers-jev)](https://github.com/mindlace/super) - Claude Code plugin marketplace whose `superpowers-jev` plugin adds optional TypeSafe Jev typed judgments at 13 decision points (shadow mode, fail-open). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/mindlace-super.md).
- [super-jev](https://github.com/Kevthetech143/super-jev) - Evidence-to-action TypeScript harness: typed Jev judgments, permitted tools, verified outcomes, and JSONL journals. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/super-jev.md).
- [Supercov](https://github.com/supercorp-ai/supercov) - Code quality and test coverage for coding agents: Jev scores each source file so the agent knows what to fix first. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/supercov.md).
- [SuperLocalMemory](https://github.com/qualixar/superlocalmemory) - Local-first, governed memory for AI agents (SQLite, hybrid recall, time-aware facts) with an optional answer check that asks Laya on-device or Jev online whether the top recalled memories actually answer the question. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/superlocalmemory.md).
- [Supply Chain Hunter](https://github.com/SecOps-AG/Supply-Chain-Hunter) - Self-hosted monitor for software supply-chain attacks: diffs each new release of the top npm and PyPI packages (and lookalike names) against the previous version, has Jev answer many narrow yes/no questions about the diff as the Scout stage, and escalates only suspicious releases to triage and analyst models. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/supply-chain-hunter.md).
- [Support Ticket Router](https://github.com/khaledjamal/support-ticket-router) - FastAPI service that asks Jev three questions per support ticket in one call (team Choice, urgency Score, churn-risk Noul) and routes by expected cost of a mistake — auto, quick review or human; dashboard, review queue, offline fake backend and a Banking77 calibration benchmark of Jev vs three LLMs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/support-ticket-router.md).
- [Sureband](https://github.com/TejaPriyan/Sureband) - Split conformal prediction wrappers that turn System One (Jev/Laya/…) confidences into coverage-guaranteed prediction sets. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sureband.md).
- [Surf CLI](https://github.com/nicobailon/surf-cli) - Chrome-control CLI for AI agents with opt-in `surf semantic.*` commands: TypeSafe Jev selects the next action from Surf's allowed menu and checks whether the goal was reached. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/surf-cli.md).
- [swamp daily briefing demo (swamp workflow that grades an invented review queue with Jev)](https://github.com/webframp/swamp-daily-briefing-demo) - Self-contained example for the swamp automation tool: a "daily briefing" workflow seeds an invented GitLab-style review queue and issue list, grades every item with Jev through the `@swamp/typesafe-ai` extension plus a fan-out triage method, and renders a terminal board with urgency, recommendation and vulnerability flags; all data is fiction. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/swamp-daily-briefing-demo.md).
- [Swap, Replicate, Read the Mass (University of Málaga protocol for auditing demographic bias in System One decision models)](https://github.com/amozo-es/system-one-bias-protocol) - Audit protocol, harness and analysis from a University of Málaga paper: 1,638,000 decisions over BBQ, CrowS-Pairs and SilverSpoon across thirteen configurations, including Jev as the commercial reference. Every item runs with options swapped, configurations repeat under new seeds, and scores read the full probability distribution so abstention cannot hide bias. Adapted items are on Hugging Face (CC BY-SA 4.0). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-bias-protocol.md).
- [SWAT](https://github.com/r0075h3ll/SWAT) - Python CLI that finds domains impersonating a brand: an LLM proposes lookalike and typosquat candidates, live ones are checked via certificate logs and DNS/HTTP, then TypeSafe Jev answers yes/no on domain and homepage impersonation. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/swat.md).
- [swift-jev](https://github.com/d-date/swift-jev) - Swift library/CLI for TypeSafe Jev typed Choice/Noul/Score judgments (distinct from krzyzanowskim/TypeSafe). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/swift-jev.md).
- [Switchbite (agent and model pool recommendations by Jev)](https://github.com/BiteFoundry/switchbite) - Rust library and native MCP stdio server from BiteFoundry that recommends which worker (agent, model, or harness and settings combination) from your pool should take a task, or holds explicitly; it also validates task DAGs and normalizes outcome feedback, with Jev as the online judge. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/switchbite.md).
- [syn-horse-notifications (Cloudflare Worker that screens notifications with Jev)](https://github.com/synmux/syn-horse-notifications) - Cloudflare Worker queue consumer that logs, rate-limits, moderates and delivers notification messages to ntfy or Pushover; Jev classifies each message, checks for attempts to manipulate the screener and rates urgency. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/syn-horse-notifications.md).
- [sys1 (alvarobartt)](https://github.com/alvarobartt/sys1) - Rust System One–compatible API for open decision models such as Laya (local `/v1/systemone`). Distinct from hraness/sys1. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/alvarobartt-sys1.md).
- [sys1grep](https://github.com/uehaj/sys1grep) - Meaning-grep CLI: TypeSafe Jev scores each line against a proposition (AND/OR/NOT); multilingual; npm `@uehaj/sys1grep` (formerly `@uehaj/semgrep` / jev-semgrep). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/jev-semgrep.md).
- [sys1npc](https://github.com/SafiullahRattar/sys1npc) - Hot-swap scripted game NPC decision points with System One models (Jev / OpenAI Decisions) and confidence-gated fallbacks. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sys1npc.md).
- [System Analyzer (terminal tool that rates your machine and answers "can I run this?" with a decision model)](https://github.com/sadra-hatami/System-Analyzer) - Terminal tool that detects CPU, RAM, GPU, VRAM, disks and OS, then asks a System One decision model to rate the machine out of 100 and answer questions such as whether it can run a given task; it can use TypeSafe Jev, OpenRouter's Jev route, OpenCode Zen's free Jev tier or a local model. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-analyzer.md).
- [System One AIOps demo (a fake Kubernetes shop where a System One model sorts every alert into page, Slack or noise)](https://github.com/blacksoxx/system-one-aiops-demo) - Reproducible demo: a small shop runs on a kind cluster with Prometheus, Alertmanager and Grafana, a chaos script breaks it in seven realistic ways, and each alert plus its logs goes to a System One model that answers typed questions with probabilities. Confident, serious alerts page on-call, unsure ones go to Slack with the best guess, and confident noise is closed. Defaults to the open Laya model on your machine; the same code runs against TypeSafe Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-aiops-demo.md).
- [System One Arcade and @system-one/ai](https://github.com/desplega-ai/system-one) - Bun workspace with one typed TypeScript API over System One decision providers (TypeSafe Jev, OpenRouter, OpenAI, Cloudflare Clef, Vercel AI Gateway) and a React arcade where those models play games against each other while a server logs every call and ranks them. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-arcade.md).
- [System One Auto Tag (Obsidian)](https://github.com/holy-max/obsidian-systemone-auto-tag) - Obsidian plugin that scores each note against your vault's existing tags with Jev Noul probabilities, sorts them into write, review and ignore bands, and only writes tags you accept from a heat-map matrix. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/obsidian-systemone-auto-tag.md).
- [System One categorizer demo (Bulgarian)](https://github.com/SciScend/system-one-categorizer-demo) - Small blog-category app and side-by-side test of Jev (TypeSafe API) and open Laya multilingual on 50 labelled Bulgarian posts, with a local BgGPT model naming new categories. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-categorizer-demo.md).
- [System One Computer Use (screenshot loop agent driven by Jev)](https://github.com/brainstormity/System-One-Computer-Use) - Prototype computer-use agent in one Python file: it takes a screenshot, asks Jev what to do next (click, type, press Enter, scroll, open a site or stop) and does it with pyautogui; a small web dashboard runs tasks and shows reports. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-computer-use.md).
- [System One Connector](https://github.com/itsmostafa/system-one-connector) - Go MCP `evaluate` connector giving agents typed Jev (or local System One / Laya) judgments with probabilities for Claude Code, Codex, Hermes, and pi. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-connector.md).
- [System One for Delphi](https://github.com/danieleteti/systemone-delphi) - Single-unit Delphi client for System One models: ask choice, score and noul questions about a text and get typed answers with every option's probability, against TypeSafe's Jev API, a local Ollama model, or any OpenAI-compatible chat model through an adapter with the same interface. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-delphi.md).
- [System One for n8n (taifoon)](https://github.com/taifoon-io/n8n-nodes-system-one) - n8n community node (`@taifoon/n8n-nodes-system-one`) that sends an item and your yes/no, pick-one and score questions to a System One host such as TypeSafe Jev or Venice and returns every answer with its probabilities, so n8n IF and Switch nodes can route on them. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/taifoon-n8n-system-one.md).
- [System One Harness](https://github.com/mev0lent/system-one-harness) - Reproducible experiment asking whether TypeSafe Jev (and peers) can gate expensive LLM triage on HTTP responses without missing anomalies. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-harness.md).
- [System One Harness](https://github.com/HarnessRouter/SystemOneHarness) - Python controller: finite-action agent loops with TypeSafe Jev (OpenRouter or TypeSafe), confidence gates, and step traces. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-harness.md).
- [System One Models — Getting Started](https://github.com/gsaini/system-one-models-getting-started) - Turns the six claims of TypeSafe's Jev launch post into runnable experiments where Jev, Claude (via TypeSafe's system-one-adapter) and keyword rules answer the same typed questions and code measures latency, tokens, cost and calibration. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-models-getting-started.md).
- [System One on Snapdragon](https://github.com/esterhuizen/system-one-on-snapdragon) - Pinned snapshot of setup scripts, Jev-compatible servers and a benchmark harness for running open System One models on Snapdragon X Elite NPU/GPU/CPU and comparing them with Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-on-snapdragon.md).
- [System One Playground](https://github.com/DonaldMurillo/system-one-playground) - SysOneScript CLI/VS Code plus Go TypeSafe System One client and Studio; start offline, add live Jev when needed. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-playground.md).
- [System One routing for Apache APISIX](https://github.com/surak/semantic-routing-llm-plugin) - Adds a `system_one` load-balancing algorithm to Apache APISIX's ai-proxy-multi plugin: a System One model (Jev, Clef) ranks the configured LLM instances for each prompt and the request goes to the highest-ranked healthy one. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/apisix-system-one-routing.md).
- [System One vs classical ML (customer-triage benchmark of Laya against logistic regression and XGBoost)](https://github.com/dursunkoc/system-one-vs-classical-ml-demo) - Controlled benchmark on 3,000 synthetic Turkish customer messages: five triage decisions handled by classical ML (logistic regression, XGBoost) and by the Laya System One model, zero-shot and fine-tuned, with F1, calibration error and reliability diagrams. Laya uses the Jev question format (choice, score, noul). Includes the data generator, scripts and a write-up. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/laya-vs-classical-ml.md).
- [System-1 decision benchmark](https://github.com/Sj0605-DataSci/system1-decision-benchmark) - Common-ground benchmark of 50 System-1 decision-model configurations — Jev, Clef, Clef-flash, Kev, Nimble and Tev1 against embedding, reranker, NLI and prompted-LLM baselines — on 22 BTZSC datasets (500 test items each), with saved scores, a living report and a paper draft. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system1-decision-benchmark.md).
- [system-one (asynq-io)](https://github.com/asynq-io/system-one) - Vendor-neutral Python SDK for typed System One decisions (hosted TypeSafe/OpenRouter or local ONNX). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/asynq-io-system-one.md).
- [system-one skill (magnus919)](https://github.com/magnus919/agent-skills) - Agent Skills-format skill for designing, integrating, evaluating and troubleshooting typed System One decisions with TypeSafe Jev (and open models such as Laya), keeping policy and side effects in code. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/magnus919-system-one-skill.md).
- [system-one-chess](https://github.com/dperezcabrera/system-one-chess) - Browser chess vs TypeSafe Jev (Vercel AI Gateway or OpenRouter) with Stockfish analysis; optional local Laya (GPL-3.0). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-chess.md).
- [system-one-decision-bench (Jev vs chat LLMs, with confidence cascades)](https://github.com/dwayo-ai/system-one-decision-bench) - Open, reproducible benchmark from Dwayo AI comparing Jev with a chat model on banking77 routing and a guardrail set for accuracy, calibration, latency and cost, plus a confidence cascade that escalates only uncertain calls, with negative results reported. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-decision-bench.md).
- [system-one-deslop (measure the 'model accent' in AI-written prose with Jev)](https://github.com/jomi-se/system-one-deslop) - Research toolkit that has Jev answer narrow questions about each sentence (a general maxim? a three-part series? a disclaimer?), counts the habits per thousand words, compares them with pre-LLM technical posts, and packages a writing brief as an agent skill. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-deslop.md).
- [system-one-mcp](https://github.com/es11225599/system-one-mcp) - Small MCP server that gives agents two tools, evaluate typed Choice/Score/Noul questions and list models, against any System One backend: TypeSafe Jev, local Ollama System One models, Kev or Laya. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-mcp.md).
- [system-one-parsers](https://github.com/Engid/system-one-parsers) - Lab building a domain-free Universal Dependencies parser out of many small Jev questions — one Choice per word for part of speech, then head and relation choices — decoded into a tree in plain code, with offline oracle/dry clients, recorded runs and a rules-only floor; work in progress. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-parsers.md).
- [system-one-reviewer](https://github.com/equationalapplications/system-one-reviewer) - Local PR reviewer: deterministic diff plumbing plus TypeSafe Jev (or local System One) judgments, with a precision/recall evaluation harness. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-reviewer.md).
- [system-one-security](https://github.com/ankushchadha/system-one-security) - Rerunnable security experiments on System One models (TypeSafe Jev, Cloudflare Clef): truncation, injection, poisoning, tripwires. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-security.md).
- [System1Router (automatic reasoning effort for Claude Code and OpenCode, with an optional Jev classifier)](https://github.com/Valkea/System1Router) - Sets the reasoning effort of every prompt you send to Claude Code or OpenCode, from low to max, by reading the prompt with recent context; the default classifier is a small local Ettin model (no key, nothing sent), and Jev can be selected as a hosted classifier or second opinion. It keeps your model and prompt cache and fails open. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system1router.md).
- [system2 (idiomatic Rust SDK for Jev with typed questions and derive macros)](https://github.com/jhartquist/system2) - Rust SDK for TypeSafe's System One API: declare yes/no, choice and score questions with macros and a `Levels` derive on your own enums, ask them against a state in one call, and get typed answers with helpers such as `is_likely()` and `most_likely()`. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system2.md).
- [systemone (gray plugin)](https://github.com/vstaln/systemone) - Rust sidecar plugin for the gray agent that puts System One decision models (TypeSafe Jev, Laya via Ollaya, any `/v1/systemone` server) behind `judge` and `semantic_find` tools plus a `/s1` chat command. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-gray.md).
- [systemone (justintout)](https://github.com/justintout/systemone) - Unofficial Go System One/Jev client with compile-time typed Choice/Noul/Score (distinct from TypeSafe Go / go-jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-justintout.md).
- [SystemOne for .NET (typed client for System One decision models)](https://github.com/orlys/systemone) - Typed .NET client for System One decision models with noul, choice, score and box questions, one API across self-hosted servers and hosted services including TypeSafe Jev, fallback chains, reusable request templates and a mock for tests. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-dotnet.md).
- [systemone-flink (Apache Flink async operator that asks a System One model typed questions about each streamed event)](https://github.com/Karthiktiwari/systemone-flink) - Java library for Apache Flink: send candidate events, such as log lines a cheap detector flagged, to any `POST /v1/systemone` server and attach the typed answers (choice, score, yes/no with probabilities) to each record. Endpoint, model, questions, retries, cache and timeouts are configuration, and timeouts or HTTP errors come out as failed decisions instead of failing the job. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-flink.md).
- [systemone-mcp-server (MCP server exposing System One decision tools over files and gated commands to Claude Code)](https://github.com/paulrobello/systemone-mcp-server) - MCP server with six tools built from levels 8-10 of ten-levels-of-jev: ask one typed question over a short state, up to 20 files and one gated read-only command, ask the same questions across many files in parallel, pick which file to open first, and single-file yes/no, choice and score checks. Commands pass a bash safety gate inside the tool. Calls go through a local cc-router relay by default, or directly to OpenRouter. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-mcp-server.md).
- [systemone-poc](https://github.com/joskr2/systemone-poc) - POC wiring Jev/Laya as a pre-routing decision layer for Pi/OpenCode/DSH agents, with measured cost notes. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-poc.md).
- [systemone-probe](https://github.com/mokko/systemone-probe) - Conformance and behaviour test harness for System One endpoints (TypeSafe Jev or self-hosted Jev-like models): checks the answer contract, Python-computed accuracy, calibration, determinism, batching, robustness and limits. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-probe.md).
- [systemone-qwen (self-hosted Jev-compatible decision API backed by a Qwen model on llama-server)](https://github.com/dohr-michael/systemone-qwen) - Small server that answers the same `/v1/systemone` wire format as Jev from a Qwen model on llama-server: each option gets a letter and its probability is the model's first-token logprob, so a Jev client switches by changing only the base URL. The author reports 0.827 on JevBench's 231 public tasks for Qwen3.5-9B against Jev's 0.857. Not affiliated with TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-qwen.md).
- [systemone-vision-test (Landolt-ring eye-chart test for Jev and local System One models)](https://github.com/naraba/systemone-vision-test) - Japanese-documented benchmark that shows Landolt-ring eye-chart images, degraded by simulated vision modes, to System One models (local `clef` and `clef-flash`, or hosted Jev) and measures down to which acuity each model still answers the ring's direction, with a GUI and measured direction-bias notes. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/systemone-vision-test.md).
- [SystemOneClient](https://github.com/schainks/system_one_client) - Elixir client for System One decision models (TypeSafe Jev, Cloudflare Clef, OpenRouter decisions, self-hosted `/v1/systemone` servers) that returns full probability distributions for Choice, Score and Noul questions, with a test stub, retries honouring `Retry-After` and telemetry that does not leak state or keys. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/system-one-client.md).
- [Séance collusion detection (hard-negative benchmark for LLM collusion monitors, with Jev as one monitor)](https://github.com/jvargas2/seance-collusion-detection) - Proposed benchmark of hard negatives for LLM collusion monitors, built for an Apart Research sprint: four LLM players play a guessing game where a secret pair may pass information through ordinary talk, and monitors, including Jev asked plainly and with a plus-factor briefing, must say which pair colludes or none; an explorer shows every game. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/seance-collusion-detection.md).
- [Tacet](https://github.com/suranjaychandra/jev-decision-layer) - Marketing decision layer that can choose to send nothing: code applies consent and frequency caps, Jev picks the next best action or budget move among what is allowed, and anything under 60% confidence goes to a person. Compares raw CRM records with compact jev-state input. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tacet.md).
- [Tacit](https://github.com/wujiajunhahah/tacit) - Local-first browser context harness: reads the page and navigation trail on your machine and hands them to Claude Code, Cursor, or any MCP agent, with a TypeSafe Jev-backed DecisionEngine (`core/jev.ts` → `/v1/systemone`). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tacit.md).
- [Talos](https://github.com/zatzk/talos) - Chat-led multi-agent orchestration TUI forked from thurbox: conversational routing, tmux worker panes, a kanban board and spec-driven execution, with a built-in Jev decision engine that powers the Auto model/agent selection. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/talos.md).
- [tandem (paired planner and builder agents under Herdr, with an optional Jev judge)](https://github.com/emsnoel/tandem) - Herdr harness where Claude Code plans, a builder agent (Codex, Claude, Copilot or OpenCode) implements in the same checkout, and a separate reviewer checks every plan and task; an optional Jev judge, pinned to `jev-1.13.0`, gives typed verdicts on handoffs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tandem-herdr.md).
- [Taste Lint](https://github.com/mblode/taste-lint) - Catch AI-sloppy UI motion, copy, and typography before you ship; optional TypeSafe Jev under-review notes via Vercel AI Gateway or direct. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/taste-lint.md).
- [taurus-jev-sdk-go](https://github.com/KKloudTarus/taurus-jev-sdk-go) - Unofficial stdlib Go client for TypeSafe System One/Jev with validated responses and masked credentials (distinct from jmelahman). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/taurus-jev-sdk-go.md).
- [tax-doc-classifier](https://github.com/kyotofin/tax-doc-classifier) - Classifies tax PDF pages into IRS forms and page kinds with TypeSafe Jev Choice over a shipped criteria JSON (261 forms). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tax-doc-classifier.md).
- [tdd-gate](https://github.com/bensheridan/tdd-gate) - Keeps test and code agents on one plan: TypeSafe Jev judges coverage, blame, gaming, weakening, and drift. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tdd-gate.md).
- [technique-guard (prompt-injection and jailbreak guard that judges the technique, with Jev)](https://github.com/j-pollack/technique-guard) - Flags user input that tries to prompt-inject or jailbreak a downstream LLM before it reaches the model: it asks Jev a set of yes/no and multiple-choice questions about the technique, not the request, and combines the answers with a frozen logistic regression into allow, review or block, with a probability. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/technique-guard.md).
- [telegram-resender (Telegram client that forwards matching messages, with an optional Jev categories gate)](https://github.com/popstas/telegram-resender) - Telegram client that runs under your own user account (Telethon, not a Bot API bot), watches chats, channels and folders, and forwards matching messages to a target chat, forum topic or webhook with a link back to the source. Triggers include word lists, OpenAI prompt matches and reactions; an optional categories gate classifies each message with one Jev Choice question. Read-only: it never sends messages on your behalf. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/telegram-resender.md).
- [telltale-systemone](https://github.com/christian-taillon/telltale-systemone) - Reference analyzer extension for the Telltale agent-monitoring tool that asks Jev narrow typed questions about agent actions from configurable guard packs, beside Telltale's deterministic rules. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/telltale-systemone.md).
- [Temporal Agent Harness](https://github.com/temporal-community/temporal-agent-harness) - Temporal-native durable multi-agent harness whose optional `jev` extra provides `agent.jev_evaluator`, a Jev-backed auto mode for tool approvals. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/temporal-agent-harness.md).
- [Ten Levels of Jev](https://github.com/disler/ten-levels-of-jev) - Teaching lab: ten incremental Jev levels from a smart if-statement to a pi coding agent that reaches for Jev on its own (offline mocks + live OpenRouter). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/ten-levels-of-jev.md).
- [TensorSharp](https://github.com/zhongkaifu/TensorSharp) - Native .NET GGUF inference engine and agent runtime that serves a Jev-compatible `POST /v1/systemone` with DiffusionGemma, answering typed questions over text, images, documents, video frames and transcripts; `jev-latest` is an alias for the local model, not hosted Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tensorsharp.md).
- [Terraform Plan Verdict](https://github.com/nandotorres/terraform-plan-verdict) - GitHub Action that turns a Terraform plan into a graded LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH / CRITICAL risk verdict across blast radius, destructiveness and security impact, surfaced as a job summary, sticky PR comment, labels and outputs; the default provider is deterministic rules, and the optional `systemone` provider asks Jev (`jev-latest`) for calibrated scores from a redacted plan summary. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/terraform-plan-verdict.md).
- [Tessera (single-owner desktop agent harness with memory, and optional Jev routing and risk checks)](https://github.com/MaverickKB/Tessera-CT) - Python standard-library agent harness for one owner: captured turns and tool outcomes, AetherMind memory over MCP with a durable outbox, shell hooks that can block a tool call, optional swarm jobs and realtime voice. With Jev enabled it picks direct, tools or delegate routes, takes part in shell risk checks and memory recall, and logs each decision's probabilities and latency. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tessera-agent-harness.md).
- [Testimonial miner](https://github.com/AppitStudio/testimonial-miner) - Python CLI that finds quotable user praise in Gmail mailboxes with one Jev request per email (message kind, app, praise quality, and a Noul per sentence) and stores verbatim quotes for review; requires a TypeSafe key and Google app passwords, and sends cleaned email text to TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/testimonial-miner.md).
- [TetraJev](https://github.com/FeiLiuEM/tetrajev) - Zero-training local decision layer: two frozen readers × four readings per item, fit-free fusion and agreement routing, with published coverage–accuracy across eight suites and a RAG reranking pass; independent of hosted Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tetrajev.md).
- [Tetris Arena (SAP-RPT-1 vs. Jev vs. LLMs vs. a heuristic on the same pieces, with live costs)](https://github.com/arndth/tetrisarena) - Browser Tetris tournament where SAP-RPT-1, Jev, any LLM and a 15-line El-Tetris heuristic play the same seeded pieces from the same candidate moves while cost and latency are tracked live; the write-up compares three briefings for Jev and finds a well-briefed cheap model and the heuristic play about as well as anything else. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tetris-arena.md).
- [Tetris: You vs Jev (play Tetris against a Jev-assisted opponent)](https://github.com/shuenrui/tetris-vs-jev) - Two-board Tetris in the browser where you play against a Jev-assisted planner on the same seeded 7-bag sequence, with garbage lines for clears, three difficulty levels and an offline planner that needs no key; a small local Python server forwards moves to TypeSafe with your key held only in page memory. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tetris-vs-jev.md).
- [tev1 (Together)](https://github.com/togethercomputer/tev1) - Open-weight Jev-inspired decision model (Qwen3.5-4B fine-tune) with training recipe and published results on Together. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tev1.md).
- [text-to-sql-guardrails (a local LLM writes SQL; deterministic guards and Jev decide whether it runs)](https://github.com/Guilherme-pxt/text-to-sql-guardrails) - Text-to-SQL assistant where the model that writes the query is not the one that decides whether it runs: Jev first classifies the request (answerable, needs clarification, injection), deterministic guards enforce an allowlist, read-only access and limits, and a second Jev request checks the SQL and result answer the question. Measured on 124 gold questions against an LLM alone with the policy in its prompt. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/text-to-sql-guardrails.md).
- [tf-jev](https://github.com/alexsosn/tf-jev) - Research prototype that uses TypeSafe Jev bounded decisions to fill a typed query IR, then deterministically compiles native Text-Fabric search templates; the model never writes query text. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tf-jev.md).
- [tg-bot-template (Telegram support-bot template that answers from your docs, with Jev for intent and bad-answer checks)](https://github.com/octrow/tg-bot-template) - Python template for a Telegram bot that answers questions about a product from its docs and code, extracted from a production bot: OpenRouter model fallback ladder, message batching, screenshot-to-text, feedback, access control and admin commands, with 878 tests. With a TypeSafe key, Jev classifies message intent (small talk gets a short answer) and judges borderline degenerate model replies; without it those checks fall back to regexes. Code comments in Russian. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tg-bot-template.md).
- [tg-x-copilot (Telegram to X drafting copilot)](https://github.com/stackoracode/tg-x-copilot) - Telegram bot that turns forwarded posts into original X drafts: Jev triages each post with typed value, type and risk questions so confident low-value posts are skipped early, then an LLM rewrites the rest and you approve and post yourself. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tg-x-copilot.md).
- [The Jev-enator](https://github.com/jakenbear/the-jev-enator) - Claude Code hooks using TypeSafe Jev for a danger gate, failure notice, and optional completion check (cassette-tested offline). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/the-jev-enator.md).
- [The right model for each step (experiment: Jev picks the skill, Claude fetches it)](https://github.com/ilanvys/jev-mekomil-study) - Small experiment on 53 Hebrew questions from the mekomIL skill catalog: Jev makes three short decisions (is a skill needed, which category, which skill) before Claude Sonnet 5.5 fetches it, compared with Claude doing it alone; the author reports median time to the right skill falling from 5.1 s to 1.5 s, with a bilingual results page. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/jev-mekomil-study.md).
- [thegreatfirewallofchina (sandbox and monitor for agents on your own compute, with a Jev trace check)](https://github.com/AnthonyHerman/thegreatfirewallofchina) - Sandbox and egress monitor for an agent running on your machine: a destination policy always denies metadata services and blocks published object-storage and cloud-API hosts, and one Jev System One call over the whole trace scores phone-home, reasoning mismatch and similar risks, after which code picks allow, review or block; Jev cannot overrule a static block. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/thegreatfirewallofchina.md).
- [Theme Tab Filter (jev-tab-filter)](https://github.com/mpeddicord/jev-tab-filter) - Chrome extension: type a theme; TypeSafe Jev scores open tabs so you can group, hide, window, or close matches. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/jev-tab-filter.md).
- [thinkdial](https://github.com/DmitryBMsk/thinkdial) - Claude Code mod: TypeSafe Jev sets per-task reasoning effort for the main loop, subagents, and Codex delegations (effort-only by default). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/thinkdial.md).
- [ThinkThen](https://github.com/botassembly/thinkthen) - CLI and SDK for typed questions about text: `decide`, `choose` and `score` return values and exit codes a shell can branch on, with TypeSafe Jev as one backend. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/thinkthen.md).
- [Thorkell (Valheim companion)](https://github.com/lofcgi/thorkell) - Research project: a Valheim companion that joins your world as a real second player, reads its own game screen, takes chat requests like "make us an axe" or "follow me", and uses Jev for fast typed decisions under a deadline while a larger model plans and talks. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/thorkell-valheim.md).
- [Tickmark Research (cited financial research with a measured Jev source router)](https://github.com/J-A-Y-0/tickmark-financial-research) - Research assistant for financial analysts that splits a question into parts, routes each to SEC filings, internal research or the web, enforces role-based access before retrieval, and checks every figure and citation against its source; the router is swappable between an LLM, Jev and a Jev-first cascade, each evaluated in the repo. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tickmark-research.md).
- [Tidepool](https://github.com/tidepool-heavy-industries/tidepool) - Tidepool/Exomonad: a live Haskell notebook and programmable agent harness where reasoning LLMs write procedures and Jev supplies cheap typed System 1 judgments inside ordinary code; ships a Jev skill for question batteries and settlement policies. Source available (PolyForm Shield). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tidepool.md).
- [tiergear](https://github.com/jang2162/tiergear) - Claude Code plugin and CLI where a decision model judge — TypeSafe Jev by default, or local Laya/Kev — picks a tier (trivial → max) for each typed prompt and maps it to a model and reasoning effort, with floors, ceilings and cache-aware switching. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tiergear.md).
- [Timeplus decision UDF (System One decisions inside streaming SQL)](https://github.com/timeplus-io/decision-udf-poc) - Proof of concept from Timeplus: a Python UDF inside Timeplus Enterprise calls a System One decision model on streaming windows, with two pipelines for video QoE incident triage and edge DDoS response, and a scorecard against rules. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/timeplus-decision-udf.md).
- [tink-route](https://github.com/jon-devlapaz/tink-route) - Confidence-aware Agent Skills router: TypeSafe Jev Noul/Choice gate, optional Tink install/prune. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tink-route.md).
- [Tiny Bouncer](https://github.com/radupopescu/tiny-bouncer) - Screens shell commands that LLM agents request in the OpenCode V2 harness: each command goes to TypeSafe Jev (default) for a judgment, and code thresholds turn the probabilities into allow, deny or the normal interactive prompt; it fails safe to asking and never grants permission your config denies. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tiny-bouncer.md).
- [Tiny World (creature island and town simulation where residents choose actions with Jev or OpenAI Decisions)](https://github.com/hank-go1/tiny-world) - Interactive simulation: give a world a direction ("Pip, go to the pond", "a thunderstorm arrives") and watch its residents decide. A creature island with ten personalities and twelve actions, and a larger town with road-following residents and an occasions calendar; each decision comes from Jev or OpenAI Decisions, with an API inspector showing tokens and cost. Automatic decisions are capped at 30 calls per session. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tiny-world.md).
- [tinycomputer](https://github.com/tinyhumansai/tinycomputer) - Rust TinyBus module for desktop (a11y) and Chrome automation: primitives, plain-language flows, and tasks that ask TypeSafe Jev many small questions. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tinyhumansai-tinycomputer.md).
- [TinyJev](https://github.com/ankit-aglawe/tinyjev) - Offline ~0.6B System One–compatible Choice/Noul/Score model on MLX or PyTorch (independent of hosted Jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tinyjev.md).
- [todo-jev](https://github.com/maker-KK/todo-jev) - Task classifier and 3-tier router (local rule / Jev skill / foundation model) with skill profiles and offline fallback. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/todo-jev.md).
- [token-saver (pick the cheapest Claude model and effort with Jev)](https://github.com/minBuild/token-saver) - Python CLI and library that routes each prompt to the cheapest Claude model and effort level that can handle it: one Jev call scores task type, difficulty and sensitivity, and transparent rules turn that into a model and effort pick. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/claude-token-saver.md).
- [tokengate (jev-model-tokengate)](https://github.com/Thanh-Mathieu95/jev-model-tokengate) - Streaming OpenAI-compatible proxy that buffers tokens and runs TypeSafe Jev safety criteria before release, aiming for zero UI leakage vs post-hoc redaction. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/jev-model-tokengate.md).
- [ToolDiscoveryBench](https://github.com/SarathChandraBellam/ToolDiscoveryBench) - Benchmark of how accurately and quickly a router picks the right MCP tool: the same golden questions run against real MCP tool catalogs (with seeded distractors at several catalog sizes) through TypeSafe Jev (flat, factored and hierarchical strategies), Strands Agents LLM tool calling, BM25 and embeddings, reporting accuracy, latency, cost and Jev calibration (ECE, Brier). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tooldiscoverybench.md).
- [toolgate](https://github.com/RiskAverseTech/toolgate) - Open Claude Code PreToolUse and MCP tool-call firewall with static rules, TypeSafe Jev judgments, YAML policy, and a local audit log. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/toolgate.md).
- [toolhunch](https://github.com/dfm88/toolhunch) - Python library for agents with too many tools: hybrid search narrows a tool catalog, then a fast decider — TypeSafe Jev, Cloudflare Clef, or Jev-compatible local servers (Strands Decider, rizzo-flow, Laya) — or an LLM picks one tool or answers 'none'; maintainer benchmarks on 40–101-tool catalogs and ToolRet's 44,453 tools, with Pydantic AI integration. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/toolhunch.md).
- [toolscout (Claude Code mod: compact MCP tool catalog and Jev routing per message)](https://github.com/adapt-to-it/toolscout) - Claude Code mod for sessions with many MCP servers: it replaces the long deferred-tool list with one line per server, asks Jev on every message which tool groups are needed and adds a short hint, and calibrates its group definitions and threshold against Jev at session start; prompts and output are in Italian. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/toolscout.md).
- [TRA / bitra (trading research agents where models give opinions and fixed rules price, size and gate every trade, with a Jev provider)](https://github.com/BijectiveLabs/bitra) - Trading research agents (`bitra` on PyPI, runs as `tra`): language models read the market and give an opinion; the system itself, with no model involved, fetches data, computes every figure, applies your limits, sizes the trade and either records it or sends one order with the stop attached. Every decision and refusal is kept on disk with its reason, and an arena replays policies over a recorded archive. Jev is one of the supported decision providers. Early release. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/bitra-trading-research-agents.md).
- [Trace Variance Analyzer (flag likely failed agent runs from one run, with one Jev read)](https://github.com/BeakrHub/TraceVarianceAnalyzer) - Scores AI agent runs you already have, with no answer key: Jev reads the task, the visible steps and the final answer once and returns the probability the answer is right; an optional pairing step uses an LLM judge to compare two runs of the same task. Includes the author's measurements on four frontier models and an evaluate command to check the scores on your own labelled sample. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/trace-variance-analyzer.md).
- [TraceRoot](https://github.com/traceroot-ai/traceroot) - Open-source agent tracing and improvement platform that auto-instruments TypeSafe Jev SDK calls as decision spans (model version, questions, answers, probabilities, cost) and uses Jev in its detection worker. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/traceroot.md).
- [TradingAgents (multi-agent LLM trading research framework with optional Jev screening of social posts)](https://github.com/TauricResearch/TradingAgents) - Multi-agent financial trading research framework (analyst, researcher, trader and risk-manager agents). Since v0.5.1 the Sentiment Analyst can screen StockTwits and Reddit posts with Jev before reading them, dropping posts that are not about the company and counting stances only when Jev is not genuinely uncertain. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tradingagents.md).
- [train-jev](https://github.com/shibing624/train-jev) - Open training recipe for JEV-style System 1 models on Qwen3.5 with LoRA decision head and System One-compatible serve. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/train-jev.md).
- [Traits](https://github.com/crosshatch/traits) - Reusable coding preferences for AI agents: feedback becomes Markdown trait files in `.agents/traits/`, and the agent proposes, gets approval for, and checks its work against them over MCP, with TypeSafe available as a model provider. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/traits.md).
- [Trax .NET](https://github.com/TraxSharp/Trax) - Headless .NET web framework that records every run in Postgres; its `Trax.Effect.Decisions.SystemOne` package adds System One decisions pinned to models such as `jev-1.13.0`. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/trax-net.md).
- [triagedy](https://github.com/m0rphtail/triagedy) - UNIX-filter security-alert triage: JSONL in, typed TypeSafe Jev decisions out; policy routing stays in Rust code. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/triagedy.md).
- [Tribar Connector for TypeSafe Jev (WordPress plugin for typed answers about content)](https://github.com/juanlentino/tribar-connector-for-typesafe-jev) - WordPress connector for TypeSafe's System One API that gives themes and plugins a typed way to ask about content and get a probability, a named choice or a score to branch on, instead of parsing prose; version 0.x, pending review for the WordPress plugin directory. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tribar-connector-typesafe-jev.md).
- [trirouter](https://github.com/hajdu-patrik/trirouter) - One prompt router for Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity (formerly jev-router): JEV — via a TypeSafe token or an OpenRouter key — classifies each prompt's task and difficulty to pick the model tier and adds a destructive-command verdict on top of a regex safety net; a built-in local classifier with the same answer format is the fallback. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/trirouter.md).
- [Truffler](https://github.com/kieranklaassen/truffler) - Rails gem for intent search: TypeSafe Jev builds index-time labels and query understanding on top of your keyword/embedding search, with optional streamed reranking. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/truffler.md).
- [tsjev for DuckDB](https://github.com/Querio-App/duckdb-jev) - DuckDB extension from Querio that adds SQL functions to classify, filter, and score table rows with TypeSafe Jev, with deduplication of identical rows, memory and disk caching, and 32 concurrent requests. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tsjev-duckdb.md).
- [Type Cascades for DGA Detection (paper code comparing 13 detectors, including Jev, on unseen malware families)](https://github.com/reypapin/dga-type-cascades) - Code, data and results for a paper on detecting malware domain-generation algorithms on 27 family groups unseen in training, judged on F1, false-positive rate and time per domain together: a fast detector decides most domains and only doubtful ones reach a transformer; variants ask a decision model (d1-3B or Jev) instead of the dictionary rule or as a doubtful-case judge. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/dga-type-cascades.md).
- [Typed Evals](https://github.com/TrustifAI/typed_evals) - Evaluate LLM/RAG/agent outputs with TypeSafe Jev judges, optional calibration, and tool guards. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typed-evals.md).
- [typed-lm](https://github.com/neurono-ml/typed-lm) - Rust Candle stack: turn dense LLMs into a Jev-style typed Choice/Noul/Score API (LoRA/QLoRA, /v1/systemone)—independent of hosted Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typed-lm.md).
- [TypeSafe (Swift)](https://github.com/krzyzanowskim/TypeSafe) - SwiftPM client for TypeSafe System One Noul/Choice/Score questions, aligned with the official JS SDK shape. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-swift.md).
- [TypeSafe .NET SDK (mehdihadeli)](https://github.com/mehdihadeli/typesafe-dotnet-sdk) - Community C# SDK for TypeSafe System One (netstandard2.0/net10.0): typed Noul/Choice/Score questions and answers; project reference until its NuGet ID is resolved. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/mehdihadeli-typesafe-dotnet-sdk.md).
- [TypeSafe AI for Agent Zero](https://github.com/3clyp50/a0-typesafe-ai) - Agent Zero plugin for typed Jev Choice/Noul/Score judgments with probability cards; bundles the official TypeSafe agent skill. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/a0-typesafe-ai.md).
- [TypeSafe AI unofficial guide (typesafe-docs)](https://github.com/typesafe-docs/typesafeai) - Unofficial guide to the TypeSafe System One API (Choice/Score/Noul, 18 cookbooks, Python + TypeScript examples, mock server). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafeai-unofficial-guide.md).
- [TypeSafe AI, from fundamentals to use cases (notebooks)](https://github.com/rickysullivan/typesafe-tutorial) - Deno Jupyter notebooks that teach Jev from the basics up: what a System One model is, Choice, Score and Noul, structure and confidence, then the four documented patterns (fan-out, confidence-gated routing, composite scoring, intent routing) and cookbooks. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/rickysullivan-typesafe-tutorial.md).
- [TypeSafe C++ SDK](https://github.com/pewriebontal/typesafe-sdk-cpp) - C++20 client for TypeSafe System One typed Choice/Score/Noul questions (Jev-ready). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-sdk-cpp.md).
- [TypeSafe Go](https://github.com/stacklok/typesafe-go) - Unofficial Go System One client (Stacklok): explicit auth options, no implicit env reads; distinct from jevgo. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-go.md).
- [TypeSafe Jev custom connector](https://github.com/markus-tobler/jev-custom-connector) - Power Platform managed custom connector for TypeSafe Jev System One (Power Automate / Copilot Studio). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/jev-custom-connector.md).
- [TypeSafe Jev × Salesforce](https://github.com/divingsbysangam/typesafe-jev-salesforce) - Salesforce DX project that calls Jev from Apex for Service Cloud case triage (classify, score, route) and as an Agentforce guardrail that screens each guest message before the agent may act, with a switchable OpenAI Decisions provider for comparison. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-jev-salesforce.md).
- [TypeSafe Mario](https://github.com/fhshaik/typesafe-mario) - Selects emulator controller inputs with Jev from structured Mario telemetry; includes a synthetic state demo, with upstream licensing unspecified. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-mario.md).
- [TypeSafe MCP](https://github.com/itsmostafa/typesafe-mcp) - Exposes typed Jev questions to MCP clients and pi, preserving provider responses with configurable direct or OpenRouter access. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-mcp.md).
- [Typesafe routing proxy (decision fast path for Home Assistant voice)](https://github.com/ksmarty/typesafe-openai-proxy) - OpenAI-compatible proxy that sits behind the hass_local_openai_llm Home Assistant integration: one System One decision per request turns clear commands and state questions into Home Assistant tool calls, asks before risky actions, and forwards everything else unchanged to your LLM server. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-openai-proxy.md).
- [TypeSafe Unofficial CLI (Jev from the terminal, TypeSafe or OpenRouter)](https://github.com/gajaguar/typesafe-cli) - Unofficial command-line interface for TypeSafe System One models: log in per provider profile, then ask noul, choice or score questions about some text from flags or a JSON or YAML questions file, on TypeSafe or OpenRouter. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/gajaguar-typesafe-cli.md).
- [typesafe-agent-gates](https://github.com/ThiagaoBR/typesafe_agent_gates) - LangChain/Deep Agents middleware using TypeSafe Jev for shell gates, issue triage, MR detection, and test-spec review. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-agent-gates.md).
- [typesafe-ai PHP client](https://github.com/basi/typesafe-ai-php-client) - Unofficial PHP 8.4 client for the Jev System One API: typed request objects for Noul, Choice and Score (plus experimental Box with images), typed responses with confidence and usage, and a mock HTTP client for tests. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-ai-php-client.md).
- [typesafe-api (Rust)](https://github.com/noahbclarkson/typesafe-api-rs) - Ergonomic Rust client for TypeSafe System One typed questions and answers (`typesafe-api` 0.1.0). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-api-rs.md).
- [TypeSafe-as-a-Judge](https://github.com/E-FL/typesafe-as-a-judge) - Unofficial Codex/Claude Code MCP plugin for bounded TypeSafe Jev route/rank/extract/verify/judge tools with proceed/review gates. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-as-a-judge.md).
- [typesafe-cli](https://github.com/y0usaf/typesafe-cli) - Shell `jev` CLI for TypeSafe Jev noul/choice/score answers as numbers (distinct from Python typesafeai-cli). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-cli.md).
- [typesafe-client (haileyok)](https://github.com/haileyok/typesafe-client) - Unofficial Go and Rust clients for TypeSafe System One (Jev): Noul/Choice/Score helpers, official-style retries, and env configuration—alongside the official Python/JS SDKs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/haileyok-typesafe-client.md).
- [typesafe-computer-use](https://github.com/awlevin/typesafe-computer-use) - Combines local OCR and Accessibility observations with Jev decisions to operate macOS, with an optional writing model. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-computer-use.md).
- [typesafe-computer-use-win](https://github.com/Vatsa10/typesafe-computer-use-win) - Windows port of typesafe-computer-use: UI Automation + OCR with TypeSafe Jev decisions (`winclicker`). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-computer-use-win.md).
- [typesafe-decisions](https://github.com/kaischinnen/typesafe-decisions) - Typed Python helpers for semantic scoring, classification, and routing on top of TypeSafe System One (`TypeSafeClient`), with docs site examples. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-decisions.md).
- [typesafe-gates](https://github.com/mpointer/typesafe-gates) - TypeScript decision gates for LLM pipelines on Jev: define Score, Choice or yes/no questions per item, get probability tables, and keep the keep-rule, threshold and fail-open or fail-closed policy in your own code. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-gates.md).
- [typesafe-go (zhirschtritt)](https://github.com/zhirschtritt/typesafe-go) - Unofficial idiomatic Go SDK for TypeSafe System One; distinct from listed stacklok/typesafe-go. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/zhirschtritt-typesafe-go.md).
- [typesafe-haystack (Haystack integration)](https://github.com/deepset-ai/haystack-core-integrations/tree/main/integrations/typesafe) - Official Haystack integration (`typesafe-haystack`) adding two pipeline components that call TypeSafe System One models such as Jev: `TypeSafeDocumentClassifier` answers typed `choice` / `score` / `noul` questions about each document and stores the probabilities in its metadata, and a text router sends inputs down pipeline branches by Jev's answer. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-haystack.md).
- [typesafe-hs (Haskell wire types and servant client for the TypeSafe System One API)](https://hackage.haskell.org/package/typesafe-hs) - Haskell library on Hackage with typed request and response models, deterministic JSON encoding and lossless decoding for TypeSafe's System One API (OpenAPI 0.2.0, including box questions, inline image assets and the model listing), plus servant routes and authenticated client methods; the author also uses it in the Baikai Haskell AI library. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-hs.md).
- [typesafe-mcp by devbux (Jev as MCP tools via npx)](https://github.com/devbux/typesafe-mcp) - Stdio MCP server published to npm that exposes Jev's choice, score, noul and box questions as typed tools, including image inputs, so any MCP client can ask Jev for calibrated decisions. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/devbux-typesafe-mcp.md).
- [typesafe-sdk-csharp (tylerwarner33)](https://github.com/tylerwarner33/typesafe-sdk-csharp) - Unofficial C# TypeSafe API client mirroring official JS/Python SDK shapes (distinct from typesafe-sdk-csharp/typesafe-sdk and CMaintz/jev-dotnet). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/tylerwarner33-typesafe-sdk-csharp.md).
- [typesafe-sdk-go (jmelahman)](https://github.com/jmelahman/typesafe-sdk-go) - Unofficial stdlib-only Go client for TypeSafe System One with Noul/Choice/Score helpers. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-sdk-go-jmelahman.md).
- [typesafe-sdk-moonbit (community MoonBit SDK for System One with verified confidence gates)](https://github.com/huanguan-Q/typesafe-sdk-moonbit) - Community MoonBit SDK for TypeSafe's System One API with typed Noul, Choice and Score questions, confidence gates, token budgets and retry backoff whose contracts are proved with `moon prove` (Why3 and Z3), plus network-free testing; it states it is independent and not affiliated with TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-sdk-moonbit.md).
- [typesafe-sdk-rust (zchee)](https://github.com/zchee/typesafe-sdk-rust) - Unofficial async Rust SDK for TypeSafe System One (`typesafe-sdk-rust` on crates.io): `#[derive(QuestionSet)]`, port of typesafe-sdk-python 0.7.1. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/zchee-typesafe-sdk-rust.md).
- [TypeSafe.AI (.NET SDK)](https://github.com/typesafe-sdk-csharp/typesafe-sdk) - Unofficial .NET System One client (NuGet TypeSafe.AI) with DI, resilience, and OTel; distinct from TypeSafeAI.Net. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-sdk-csharp.md).
- [TypeSafe.AI.Sdk for .NET by hardkoded (community port of the TypeSafe JavaScript SDK)](https://github.com/hardkoded/typesafe-sdk-dotnet) - Community .NET client for TypeSafe's System One API, ported from `@typesafe-ai/sdk` and maintained by Darío Kondratiuk (hardkoded), with behaviour aligned to the JavaScript SDK: typed questions and answers, NuGet package `TypeSafe.AI.Sdk`, targets net10.0 and netstandard2.0, with a documentation site written for agents. Not an official TypeSafe product. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-sdk-dotnet-hardkoded.md).
- [typesafe_rs](https://github.com/ppweni/typesafe_rs) - Unofficial async Rust client for Jev: send state and typed Choice, Score and Noul questions, attach image assets and try experimental Jev 1.14 Box questions, with typed responses and a configurable retry policy. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafe-rs.md).
- [typesafeai-cli](https://github.com/maddygoround/typesafeai-cli) - Python `typesafe` CLI for TypeSafe Jev ask/decide/screen/verify flows for humans and agents. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafeai-cli.md).
- [TypeSafeAI.Net](https://github.com/Hawxy/TypeSafeAI.Net) - Community .NET client with typed questions, dependency injection, and Microsoft.Extensions.AI adapters. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafeai-net.md).
- [typesafer (unofficial R client for the TypeSafe API)](https://github.com/reisfe/typesafer) - Unofficial in-development R package for the TypeSafe AI API: model discovery through `GET /v1/models` and evaluation through `POST /v1/systemone` with Noul, Choice and Score question constructors, timeouts, bounded retries, credential redaction and response structures kept as nested lists. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafer.md).
- [TypeSafeSharp (.NET)](https://github.com/sanamhub/typesafe-dotnet) - Unofficial .NET client for TypeSafe's System One API and Jev on `net10.0` and `netstandard2.0`: typed answers, retries and error types modeled on the official SDKs, `EvaluateManyAsync` batching, dependency injection and OpenTelemetry spans; not affiliated with TypeSafe. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typesafesharp.md).
- [TypeWright](https://github.com/Jev-Engineering/TypeWright) - Declarative compiler: state/decisions → frozen typed Jev JSON program via DSPy/GEPA (alpha; no live accuracy claims yet). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typewright.md).
- [typia (`@typia/jev`)](https://github.com/samchon/typia) - typia's `@typia/jev` package generates Jev noul/choice/score questions from TypeScript types — JSDoc comments become instructions and choice descriptions — and `evaluation.decode()` validates Jev's answers back into the declared type; works with `@typesafe-ai/sdk`, Vercel AI SDK and OpenRouter. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/typia-jev.md).
- [UIScore (UI design score from a screenshot)](https://github.com/itsdimachka/uiscore) - Mac menu-bar app that scores a screenshot's UI design on contrast, grouping, hierarchy, clarity, trust, reading grade and feeling, combining yes/no checks from design research answered by Jev with Claude, into one 0 to 100 score. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/uiscore.md).
- [unified-brain (codebase memory for coding agents, with Jev selecting the evidence)](https://github.com/DevLoops-ai/unified-brain) - Persistent codebase memory served to coding agents over MCP: it ingests git history and tree-sitter symbols into one SQLite file with text and vector indexes, and in its Jev mode scores every stored skill against the question so the evidence set is as large as the question needs, bounded by score, count and size limits. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/unified-brain.md).
- [universal-jev](https://github.com/raj8525/universal-jev) - Plugin and MCP server for coding agents that uses Jev to compress passing terminal logs into receipts and to make fast DOM decisions with a browser-use fallback. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/universal-jev.md).
- [unjangled-guardrails (local-first Jev-compatible guard for coding agent tool calls)](https://github.com/johnbuck/unjangled-guardrails) - Fork of the listed jev-guard: one hook-based guardrail that judges every tool call and result for Claude Code, Codex, Copilot CLI, Gemini CLI and Cursor using any Jev-compatible classifier, local by default, with TypeSafe and Vercel AI Gateway as cloud options and offline rules when the classifier is down. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/unjangled-guardrails.md).
- [unsafe-c-finder](https://github.com/etnt/unsafe-c-finder) - Classify C/C++ snippets and staged hunks with TypeSafe Jev through OpenRouter (unsafe probability then CWE bucket). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/unsafe-c-finder.md).
- [Urdia](https://github.com/SaulineytorX/urdia) - Local workspace for coding agents (Linux/WSL source preview) with live visibility, explicit permissions and an experimental per-task mode where Jev selects the agent/model profile. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/urdia.md).
- [use-jev](https://github.com/Kmasterrr/use-jev) - Claude Code/Codex skill: hand bounded choice/score/noul decisions to TypeSafe Jev via OpenRouter. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/use-jev.md).
- [v1-decisions-vllm](https://github.com/Timiku/v1-decisions-vllm) - Proposed vLLM `/v1/decisions` typed-decision API with Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` projection (self-hosted). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/v1-decisions-vllm.md).
- [Valen](https://github.com/Valen-Team/Valen) - Open multimodal System One–style decision models (text/image/video → candidate probabilities), with training code, 0.8B–4B weights and a free hosted demo; independent of hosted Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/valen.md).
- [vd-jev (VisiData + Jev)](https://github.com/ondata/vd-jev) - VisiData plugin that asks closed Jev questions over every cell of a text column via OpenRouter (default `typesafe/jev-1.14`) and writes typed probability/label/score columns back into the sheet. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vd-jev.md).
- [Vellum (Sanity Labs)](https://github.com/sanity-labs/vellum) - Sanity Labs experiment that maps Markdown into Sanity documents with a classifier instead of an LLM: code does everything mechanical and TypeSafe Jev answers choice and yes/no questions about which schema field each block fills; hosted playground and npm package `@sanity-labs/vellum`. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/sanity-vellum.md).
- [vev](https://github.com/Xiaooolong/vev) - Open multimodal Jev-like decision models (text+images) speaking `/v1/systemone` on Qwen3.5 weights; self-host on your GPU. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vev.md).
- [VexJoy Agent](https://github.com/notque/vexjoy-agent) - Agent toolkit for Claude Code/Codex: `/do` routes work to specialist agents/skills; optional `/d` uses TypeSafe Jev to classify and gate intent before dispatch. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vexjoy-agent.md).
- [vibecheck-linear-jev](https://github.com/mgd34msu/vibecheck-linear-jev) - Coding-agent work ledger on Linear (MCP + skills) where a Jev-compatible judgment model checks agent done/blocker reports. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vibecheck-linear-jev.md).
- [Vibefilter for Filament](https://github.com/vibefilter/filament) - Laravel Filament plugin: filter any table with a plain-English statement; TypeSafe Jev scores each row's probability that the statement is true and rows above a threshold remain. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vibefilter-filament.md).
- [VideoJev](https://github.com/MCG-NJU/VideoJev) - System One research model stack for video understanding and interaction (TimeJev decision modules). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/videojev.md).
- [vidjev](https://github.com/numinousmuses/vidjev) - System One–style decisions on video: CARLA drone following and zero-shot CCTV anomaly detection with open models (not hosted Jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vidjev.md).
- [Vigil (decision ledger and circuit breaker for agents, with a Jev judge)](https://github.com/leo-bone/vigil) - TypeScript kernel that makes every agent decision interceptable, traceable and calibratable: an orchestrator loop with stop-loss, routing, quality gates, acceptance and escalation, each fork a kernel ruling recorded in an append-only hash-chained, Ed25519-signed ledger; the judge can be TypeSafe Jev or a local `/v1/systemone` server. Chinese-language docs. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vigil-oversight.md).
- [vLLM Jev](https://github.com/Egbertjing/vllm-jev) - Native vLLM serving for Jev-style Choice/Noul/Score checkpoints over HTTP. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vllm-jev.md).
- [vLLM Jev (mode-io)](https://github.com/mode-io/vllm-jev) - Native vLLM serving for Jev-style Choice/Noul/Score checkpoints (distinct from Egbertjing/vllm-jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/mode-io-vllm-jev.md).
- [voice-intent-pragmatics-bench (Korean voice-assistant intent benchmark with a Jev memory gate)](https://github.com/kimtami/voice-intent-pragmatics-bench) - Measured benchmark of 30 Korean voice-assistant utterances across 37 conditions (3,330 responses) asking whether shared context and pragmatics cues let fast, low-effort models approach a stronger model's accuracy without adding latency; one family of conditions uses Jev as a relevance gate that passes only memories scoring above a threshold. Results page with animated charts. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/voice-intent-pragmatics-bench.md).
- [voicevox-jev-proxy](https://github.com/nemalabs/voicevox-jev-proxy) - Corrects VOICEVOX Japanese readings (optional intonation) with TypeSafe Jev; CLI WAV export or VOICEVOX-compatible API proxy. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/voicevox-jev-proxy.md).
- [Voyager (fast macOS computer use where Jev verifies every step)](https://github.com/jordanhilado/voyager) - Computer use for Mac apps: Claude Code plans a batch of steps at once, a small local daemon carries them out through macOS accessibility and ScreenCaptureKit, Jev checks each step against the screen, and safety rules in code pause before anything is sent. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/voyager-computer-use.md).
- [vulnwash (Snyk Labs)](https://github.com/snyk-labs/vulnwash-cli) - Snyk Labs triage harness that runs every Snyk CLI finding — SCA (`snyk test`) and SAST (`snyk code test`) — through TypeSafe Jev for contextual, tunable prioritization of dependency vulnerabilities and true-positive filtering of code findings, without touching Snyk's detection; cached and re-triageable at a new confidence bar with no new calls. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/vulnwash.md).
- [wagtail-jev](https://github.com/rinti/wagtail-jev) - Wagtail CMS editor buttons: TypeSafe Jev suggests page tags and rates fields on custom scales. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/wagtail-jev.md).
- [Wald-Q4B](https://github.com/org2AI/wald-4b) - Open-weight 4B decision model with calibrated probabilities and a Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` API (self-hosted; not TypeSafe Jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/wald-4b.md).
- [Waldo Bench](https://github.com/musubipapi/waldo-bench) - Can a decision model find Waldo? 66 Where's Waldo scenes cut into tiles and nine image-input decision models asked to pick his tile: Jev 1.14 found 47 (71%) with the fastest typical search, 2.6 s, behind Liquid d1-ultra's 83%. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/waldo-bench.md).
- [WaterSheep](https://github.com/SamratDuttaOfficial/WaterSheep) - Open Apache-2.0 decision model with a local Jev-compatible `/v1/systemone` server that TypeSafe's Python SDK can use via `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL`, plus multi-label questions (independent of hosted TypeSafe Jev). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/watersheep.md).
- [Wayfinder (Jev-powered site search that sends visitors straight to the right page)](https://github.com/Mou1z/wayfinder) - Drop-in site search for websites: you describe each page in visitors' words, and Jev (through OpenRouter) picks the one page that answers what someone typed, offers a few options when unsure, and falls back to word matching without a key or when the model is slow; it can only choose from your list, never write answers. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/wayfinder-search.md).
- [web-scout-ai](https://github.com/RSO9192/web-scout-ai) - Agentic Python web-research pipeline that opens and extracts the sources it finds and returns a cited answer with an audit trail; semantic classification and follow-up link selection default to TypeSafe Jev, with generative models doing extraction and synthesis. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/web-scout-ai.md).
- [webctl](https://github.com/dorkitude/webctl) - Agent web-search CLI that scores and judges multi-provider results (optional scrape chunks) with TypeSafe Jev. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/webctl.md).
- [webular (web research CLI with Jev decisions)](https://github.com/srinitude/webular) - Web research CLI that covers search, scrape, crawl, map, extract, summarize, answer and multi-step research with free, open-source parts, and uses Jev for its judgment calls, with a deterministic fallback for every decision. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/webular.md).
- [wellposed](https://github.com/suraj-phanindra/wellposed) - Zero-dependency linter for TypeSafe Jev requests: broken state paths, missing Choice escape hatches, bundled judgments (`wellposed` 0.4.0). [Project guide](community/projects/tools/wellposed.md).
- [What Changed Its Mind? (does more evidence improve Jev's election forecasts, or only make them more certain?)](https://github.com/prashaantr/what-changed-its-mind) - Open research essay on competitive 2026 US midterm races: explore a map, add evidence, inspect the exact Jev requests and compare precomputed changes when candidate or source order changes. The first frozen release has three Senate races and 23 valid forecasts; the author stresses it is an experiment, not a calibrated election forecast. [Project guide](community/projects/tools/what-changed-its-mind.md).
